       8  ## 1. Context and goal
       9  
      10 -The service holds customer money, runs as several replicas behi
         -nd a load balancer, and must stay correct and responsive when c
         -lients misbehave, when a dependency fails and while it is being
         - deployed. This spec covers the layers around the business logi
         -c: rate limits, limits on request bodies, security headers, tru
         -sted proxies, correlation ids and logs, health checks, graceful
         - shutdown, timeouts, the database connection pool, configuratio
         -n, metrics and the API documentation. It fixes the values that 
         -specs 000, 003 and 005 leave to it (SYS-R34, SYS-R35, MOV-R19, 
         -IDM-R11).                                                      
      10 +The service holds customer money, runs as several replicas behi
         +nd a load balancer, and must stay correct and responsive when c
         +lients misbehave, when a dependency fails and while it is being
         + deployed. This spec covers the layers around the business logi
         +c: rate limits, limits on request bodies, security headers, tru
         +sted proxies, correlation ids and logs, health checks, graceful
         + shutdown, timeouts, the database connection pool, configuratio
         +n, metrics, the optional error reporting and the API documentat
         +ion. It fixes the values that specs 000, 003 and 005 leave to i
         +t (SYS-R34, SYS-R35, MOV-R19, IDM-R11).                        
      11  
      12  Locally the load balancer is nginx in Docker Compose; in AWS it
           is an Application Load Balancer with AWS WAF in front (phase 1
          2-infra). PostgreSQL is the only source of truth (SYS-R16); Red
          is holds only the per-user rate-limit counters, so money correc
          tness never depends on it. Terms have the meanings in the gloss
          ary of spec 000.

      68  The load balancer reads its own variables: `RATE_LIMIT_IP_RPS`

⏺ Update(specs/007-security-ops/spec.md)
  ⎿  Added 26 lines
      133  - **Statement timeout.** A statement cancelled by `statement_t
           imeout` is a transient overload like a lock timeout, and the r
           equest has already used most of its time, so it is answered 50
           3 and not retried in process (SEC-R32). At the request timeout
            the 503 is answered first; the service starts no further stat
           ement, and the statement in flight runs until it ends, at most
            until `statement_timeout`, before the transaction is rolled b
           ack (SEC-R33). The service never cancels a statement from anot
           her connection.
      134  - **Pool size.** Each replica holds `DB_POOL_MAX` (default 10)
            request connections plus one readiness connection, and 10 con
           nections are kept in reserve for migrations, the reconcile and
            cleanup scripts and manual sessions (SEC-R36). Locally that i
           s 2 × 11 + 10 = 32 of PostgreSQL's 97 usable connections; in A
           WS the same rule applies to RDS Proxy's connection budget and 
           the task count, a deployment's surge included: the autoscaling
            maximum × the ECS service's `deployment_maximum_percent` / 10
           0, 6 × 200 / 100 = 12 tasks, so 12 × 11 + 10 = 142 of the 197 
           usable connections that the parameter group's `max_connections
           ` of 200 leaves.
      135  
      136 +### 1.10 Error reporting                                      
      137 +                                                              
      138 +Optional, and off unless `SENTRY_DSN` is set, so the demo and 
          +the tests need no key and send nothing outside the machine ([A
          +DR-0023](../../docs/adr/0023-optional-observability-off-by-def
          +ault.md)). When it is set, the service reports each unexpected
          + error, a request answered 500 with problem type `/problems/in
          +ternal-error` (SYS-R25), to the endpoint the DSN names, in the
          + Sentry envelope format that Sentry and compatible servers suc
          +h as GlitchTip accept. Every other answer is expected and is n
          +ot reported: the 4xx, the 503s of transient conditions (SYS-R3
          +4), the load balancer's own answers, and errors outside a requ
          +est, which the logs and the exit code already record (SEC-R52)
          +.                                                             
      139 +                                                              
      140 +An event is built from this allowlist, never by removing field
          +s from a richer one:                                          
      141 +                                                              
      142 +| Field                                                       
          +         | Value                                              
          +                                                      |       
      143 +| ------------------------------------------------------------
          +-------- | ---------------------------------------------------
          +----------------------------------------------------- |       
      144 +| `event_id`, `timestamp`, `platform` (`node`), `level` (`erro
          +r`)      | generated by the reporter                          
          +                                                      |       
      145 +| `environment`                                               
          +         | `NODE_ENV`                                         
          +                                                      |       
      146 +| `release`                                                   
          +         | the `version` of `package.json`                    
          +                                                      |       
      147 +| `tags.requestId`                                            
          +         | the correlation id (SYS-R21)                       
          +                                                      |       
      148 +| `tags.route`                                                
          +         | the route template, or `unmatched` (SEC-R42)       
          +                                                      |       
      149 +| `tags.method`                                               
          +         | the HTTP method                                    
          +                                                      |       
      150 +| `tags.replicaId`                                            
          +         | `REPLICA_ID`, or the host name when it is unset (DE
          +P-R14)                                                |       
      151 +| `tags.sqlstate`                                             
          +         | the SQLSTATE of a database error, only when the err
          +or has one                                            |       
      152 +| `exception.values[0].type`                                  
          +         | the error's name                                   
          +                                                      |       
      153 +| `exception.values[0].value`                                 
          +         | the error's message, scrubbed as below             
          +                                                      |       
      154 +| `exception.values[0].stacktrace.frames`                     
          +         | for each frame: the function, the file path inside 
          +the application, the line and the column; no source line and n
          +o variable |                                                  
      155 +                                                              
      156 +Nothing else is sent: no request header, so neither `Authoriza
          +tion` nor `Idempotency-Key`; no query string; no request or re
          +sponse body; no client address; no user; no breadcrumbs; and n
          +o `detail`, `hint`, `where`, `position` or query text of a dat
          +abase error. The message is scrubbed in this order: each value
          + of `JWT_SECRET`, `CURSOR_SECRET`, `PGPASSWORD`, the passwords
          + of `DATABASE_URL` and `REDIS_URL`, `SENTRY_DSN`, and the requ
          +est's own bearer token and `Idempotency-Key` becomes `[Redacte
          +d]`; each JWT-shaped token (three base64url segments joined by
          + dots) becomes `<token>`; each UUID becomes `<uuid>`; and each
          + run of decimal digits becomes `<n>`, so no amount (a digit st
          +ring, SYS-R03), account id, transaction id, token or secret re
          +aches the endpoint (SEC-R53).                                 
      157 +                                                              
      158 +Reporting never changes or delays an answer (SEC-R54): a repor
          +t is queued when the 500 is logged and sent in the background;
          + each send is abandoned after 2000 ms; at most 20 reports are 
          +pending, waiting or in flight, per replica, and a further one 
          +is dropped; a send that fails, times out or gets a status othe
          +r than 2xx drops its report. As for Redis (SEC-R06), the repor
          +ter writes one `warn` line when sends start failing and one `i
          +nfo` line when one succeeds again, never one line per report a
          +nd never the DSN. The shutdown never waits for a pending repor
          +t, which is lost when the process exits (SEC-R55).            
      159 +                                                              
      160 +In AWS the tasks have no outbound internet path (section 1.7 o
          +f spec 008), so the Terraform sets no `SENTRY_DSN` and error r
          +eporting stays off there (section 6 of spec 008).             
      161 +

      163  
      164  | ID      | Requirement (EARS)

      158  Reporting never changes or delays an answer (SEC-R54): a repor
           t is queued when the 500 is logged and sent in the background;
            each send is abandoned after 2000 ms; at most 20 reports are 
           pending, waiting or in flight, per replica, and a further one 
           is dropped; a send that fails, times out or gets a status othe
           r than 2xx drops its report. As for Redis (SEC-R06), the repor
           ter writes one `warn` line when sends start failing and one `i
           nfo` line when one succeeds again, never one line per report a
           nd never the DSN. The shutdown never waits for a pending repor
           t, which is lost when the process exits (SEC-R55).

      43  Every setting comes from an environment variable and is validat
          ed at startup (SEC-R39). Integer values are strings of decimal 
          digits without sign or leading zero, as in IDM-R23. Variables o
          f other specs keep the rules of those specs: `ACCOUNT_LOCK_TIME
          OUT_MS` (spec 003), `IDEMPOTENCY_WAIT_TIMEOUT_MS` and `IDEMPOTE
          NCY_KEY_TTL_SECONDS` (spec 005), `MAX_AMOUNT_MINOR` (spec 002),
           `JWT_SECRET`, `JWT_ISSUER` and `JWT_AUDIENCE` (spec 006). This
           spec adds:

          +eplicas' `METRICS_PORT`, inside the compose network every 5 se

          +ata inside the container for 1 day, so the data is lost with t

      31    - Error trackers are built for this job: unexpected exception
          s with stack traces, grouped into issues, counted and alerted o
          n.
      32    - An event built from an allowlist (section 1.10 of spec 007)
           sends the correlation id and the route, which lead to the full
           log lines, and nothing else.
      33  - **Cons:**
      34 -  - Two more images to pin and update, and about 300 MB to pull
         - the first time the profile runs.                              
      34 +  - Two more images to pin and update, and a few hundred MB to 
         +pull the first time the profile runs.                          
      35    - The local Prometheus is not what AWS would use (ADOT and Am
          azon Managed Service for Prometheus), so the dashboard is a loc
          al tool, not the production one.
      36    - The allowlist and the message scrub are code to maintain, a
          nd a stack trace and a scrubbed message are less than a full SD
          K event would hold.
      37    - In AWS the tasks have no outbound internet path, so error r
          eporting needs a NAT gateway with an egress allow-list before i
          t can be turned on there.

⏺ I've written the proposal into the docs. Nothing is committed and no code is
  written; I'm stopping here for your review.

  Spec 007 (error reporting)
  - Configuration: a new variable, SENTRY_DSN. Unset or empty means reporting is
    off. A malformed value stops startup, as other bad variables do, and the
    error never prints the value.
  - What is sent: section 1.10 lists every field an event may hold, and nothing
    else is sent. The tags are the correlation id, route, method, replica and
    SQLSTATE; the error part is its name, a scrubbed message and the stack
    frames.
  - Scrubbing: the message has secrets and the request's own token and key
    replaced with [Redacted]. Then JWT-shaped tokens become <token>, UUIDs
    <uuid> and runs of digits <n>.
  - No effect on answers: each send gives up after 2000 ms, at most 20 reports
    wait per replica, and failures are dropped. Logs get one warn line when
    sending starts failing and one info line when it recovers. Shutdown never
    waits for a report.
  - New requirements: SEC-R50 to SEC-R55. SEC-R22 also gets SENTRY_DSN added to
    what the logs must hide.
  - New ACs: SEC-AC40 to SEC-AC46.

  Spec 008 (observability profile)
  - Services: prometheus and grafana join table 1.1 in the profile
    observability.
  - Prometheus: scrapes api-1:9464 and api-2:9464 every 5 s inside the compose
    network and publishes no port. Its data lives in the container for one day.
  - Grafana: on 127.0.0.1:3030, anonymous viewers only, with login, basic auth
    and sign-up off. The dashboard and data source are read-only, and Grafana's
    usage reporting and update checks are off.
  - Dashboard: section 1.9 has a table with the query for each panel you listed,
    plus one showing whether both replicas are scraped.
  - New requirements: DEP-R42 to DEP-R45. DEP-R01 now also excludes prometheus
    and grafana.
  - New ACs: DEP-AC31 and DEP-AC32 (unit) and DEP-AC33 (e2e). DEP-AC01's ports
    are unchanged; its Then only adds "prometheus and grafana were not started".
  - AWS: out of scope. The tasks there can't reach the internet, so reporting
    stays off in AWS.

  Plan, tasks and ADR
  - Plans: plans 007 and 008 get new file rows, AC rows and notes for
    12b-observability.
  - Tasks: both tasks.md files get a 12b-observability section with every task
    unticked.
  - ADR-0023 (Proposed): explains why Prometheus and Grafana locally, why a
    Sentry-compatible error reporter rather than PostHog, and why both are off
    by default.
  - docs/dependencies.md: the two images are added under a new "Container
    images" section, marked as pending your approval.
  - Index and phase list: 12b-observability is added to the phase list in
    AGENTS.md, and the spec index scopes are updated.

  npm run trace passes with the 10 new ACs pending, and the format check and all
  368 unit tests pass. Two existing unit tests in compose.test.ts list every
  service and published port, so they will need updating; the plan includes
  that.

  Decisions I need from you
  1. How reports are sent (most important):
     - My own small client (recommended): writes Sentry's event format with
       Node's built-in fetch, so no new dependency and every byte sent comes
       from the allowed-field list.
     - @sentry/node 11.6: the official package. It installs 18 packages and 82
       MB, and by default it hooks into http, pg and Fastify and records request
       data, so all of that would have to be turned off.
  2. Message or type only: send the scrubbed error message, or only the error
     type and stack? I recommend the scrubbed message.
  3. Errors outside a request: report crashes that happen outside a request too?
     I recommend no, which matches your "only the 500s".
  4. Prometheus UI: publish it on 127.0.0.1:9090? I recommend no; Grafana is the
     only viewer.
  5. SENTRY_DSN in compose.yaml: pass it through as ${SENTRY_DSN:-}? I recommend
     no. A value left in the shell or a stray .env would otherwise send reports
     out without anyone asking.
  6. DEP-AC33 in CI: run it in the CI e2e job? I recommend yes; it pulls two
     images and adds about a minute.

  Once you approve, I'll record it as earlier changes were recorded: a dated
  paragraph in plan 000 section 11, ADR-0023 set to Accepted, the open-question
  rows filled in, and the images marked approved. Specs 007 and 008 stay
  Approved. Then implementation can start.

✻ Sautéed for 11m 4s · done 8:21 AM

❯ Approved, with these answers; record them as you described, and in the AC     
  texts where they apply.                                                       
  1. Your own client on Node's fetch, no new dependency.                        
  2. Send the scrubbed message.                                                 
  3. No reports for errors outside a request.                                   
  4. No published Prometheus port; the "Replicas scraped" panel and DEP-AC33    
  show the targets.                                                             
  5. No SENTRY_DSN in compose.yaml. Add an opt-in override instead,             
  compose.error-reporting.yaml, which passes SENTRY_DSN to api-1 and api-2 as   
  ${SENTRY_DSN:?...}, so it refuses to start without one, and document it in    
  the README.                                                                   
  6. Yes, DEP-AC33 runs in the CI job e2e, but never during the load test (the  
  runner has 2 CPUs): it removes prometheus and grafana when it ends, and the   
  e2e suite also checks that port 3030 is free before it starts.                
     Also: SENTRY_DSN must be https, except for a loopback host (127.0.0.1,     
  [::1] or localhost) such as the tests' fake endpoint; an http DSN to any      
  other host stops startup like a malformed one.                                
     Then implement the 12b-observability tasks, tests first, as the plan       
  describes them, including a Makefile target observability, with the images    
  pinned by version and a digest you look up (never invent one). Prove with a   
  test against a local fake endpoint that the reporter sends nothing without    
  SENTRY_DSN and, with one, sends a 500 with its correlation id and none of the 
  forbidden fields. Then verify and show me: docker compose --profile           
  observability up -d, both targets up (from Prometheus's API inside its        
  container), and the dashboard loading in Grafana with data. Run npm run       
  check, npm run test:integration and npm run trace.

      67  
      68  The load balancer reads its own variables: `RATE_LIMIT_IP_RPS` 
          (default 500) and `RATE_LIMIT_IP_BURST` (default 1000). `METRIC
          S_PORT` defaults to 9464, the port Prometheus exporters convent
          ionally use. The ranges are wide enough for tests and productio
          n and narrow enough to catch typos, such as a pool of 1000 or a
           timeout of hours.

      135  
      136  ### 1.10 Error reporting
      137  
      138 -Optional, and off unless `SENTRY_DSN` is set, so the demo and 
          -the tests need no key and send nothing outside the machine ([A
          -DR-0023](../../docs/adr/0023-optional-observability-off-by-def
          -ault.md)). When it is set, the service reports each unexpected
          - error, a request answered 500 with problem type `/problems/in
          -ternal-error` (SYS-R25), to the endpoint the DSN names, in the
          - Sentry envelope format that Sentry and compatible servers suc
          -h as GlitchTip accept. Every other answer is expected and is n
          -ot reported: the 4xx, the 503s of transient conditions (SYS-R3
          -4), the load balancer's own answers, and errors outside a requ
          -est, which the logs and the exit code already record (SEC-R52)
          -.                                                             
      138 +Optional, and off unless `SENTRY_DSN` is set, so the demo and 
          +the tests need no key and send nothing outside the machine ([A
          +DR-0023](../../docs/adr/0023-optional-observability-off-by-def
          +ault.md)). When it is set, the service reports each unexpected
          + error, a request answered 500 with problem type `/problems/in
          +ternal-error` (SYS-R25), to the endpoint the DSN names, in the
          + Sentry envelope format that Sentry and compatible servers suc
          +h as GlitchTip accept, written by the service's own client wit
          +h Node's `fetch`, without an SDK. Every other answer is expect
          +ed and is not reported: the 4xx, the 503s of transient conditi
          +ons (SYS-R34), the load balancer's own answers, and errors out
          +side a request, which the logs and the exit code already recor
          +d (SEC-R52).

      157  
      158  Reporting never changes or delays an answer (SEC-R54): a repor
           t is queued when the 500 is logged and sent in the background;
            each send is abandoned after 2000 ms; at most 20 reports are 
           pending, waiting or in flight, per replica, and a further one 
           is dropped; a send that fails, times out or gets a status othe
           r than 2xx drops its report. As for Redis (SEC-R06), the repor
           ter writes one `warn` line when sends start failing and one `i
           nfo` line when one succeeds again, never one line per report a
           nd never the DSN. The shutdown never waits for a pending repor
           t, which is lost when the process exits (SEC-R55).

      138 +**Error reporting override.** `compose.yaml` never passes `SEN
          +TRY_DSN` (section 1.10 of spec 007), so a value left in the sh
          +ell or a stray `.env` never sends a report out of the machine.

          +for the demo: it adds to `api-1` and `api-2` only `SENTRY_DSN:
          + ${SENTRY_DSN:?...}`, so `docker compose -f compose.yaml -f co
          +mpose.error-reporting.yaml up --build --wait` refuses to start
          + unless `SENTRY_DSN` is set and not empty, and the service the
          +n validates it (SEC-R39).

      1  # ADR-0023: Optional observability: Prometheus and Grafana local
         ly, a Sentry-compatible error reporter, both off by default
      2  
      3 -- **Status:** Proposed                                          
      3 +- **Status:** Accepted                                          
      4  - **Date:** 2026-10-09
      5  - **Related specs:** 007-security-ops, 008-deployment
      6  
     ...
      67  - **Its own client, writing Sentry envelopes with Node's `fetch
          `:** no new dependency; every byte sent is written by the allow
          list, so no SDK default can add a header, a body or a breadcrum
          b. It costs about 150 lines (DSN parsing, the envelope, stack f
          rames, the bounded queue of SEC-R54) and does not honour the se
          rver's rate-limit headers beyond those bounds.
      68  - **The official SDK `@sentry/node` 11.6:** maintained by Sentr
          y, with its transport, retries and the server's rate limits han
          dled. It installs 18 packages and 82 MB, and by default instrum
          ents `http`, `pg` and Fastify through OpenTelemetry and records
           request data and breadcrumbs, so each default integration must
           be turned off and every event rebuilt from the allowlist in `b
          eforeSend`. Its size also enlarges the runtime image that `triv
          y image` scans.
      69  
      70 -The recommendation is the service's own client; the owner decid
         -es.                                                            
      70 +The owner chose the service's own client on Node's `fetch`, wit
         +h no new dependency, on 2026-10-09.                            
      71  
      72  ## Decision
      73  
      74 -Chosen option: **Option A**, pending the owner's approval, beca
         -use it reuses what the service already exposes and changes noth
         -ing by default.                                                
      74 +Chosen option: **Option A**, approved by the owner on 2026-10-0
         +9, because it reuses what the service already exposes and chang
         +es nothing by default.                                         
      75  
      76  - **Prometheus and Grafana, locally.** The metrics are already 
          in the Prometheus format, so Prometheus scrapes them unchanged 
          and Grafana shows them; both run offline from pinned images ins
          ide the compose network, so `METRICS_PORT` stays internal. They
           are local because the AWS deployment has no metrics backend of
           its own yet: its alarms use AWS's metrics, and scraping in AWS
           stays the ADOT and Amazon Managed Service for Prometheus step 
          of `docs/deployment/aws.md`.
      77  - **A Sentry-compatible error reporter, not product analytics.*
          * An unexpected 500 needs an error tracker: a stack trace, grou
          ping and alerts, linked to the logs by the correlation id. A pr
          oduct-analytics tool such as PostHog is built to follow identif
          ied users and their actions, which for a money service means se
          nding customer behaviour and identifiers out, with no need for 
          it. The Sentry protocol is open and self-hostable, and only an 
          allowlisted, scrubbed event is sent.
      78 -- **Both off by default.** `docker compose up` stays exactly as
         - DEP-AC01 states, the demo and the tests need no key or network
         -, and no data leaves the machine unless the operator asks for i
         -t: the profile with `--profile observability`, error reporting 
         -by setting `SENTRY_DSN`.                                       
      78 +- **The service's own reporter.** It writes Sentry envelopes wi
         +th Node's `fetch` and adds no dependency, so every byte it send
         +s comes from the allowlist; it sends the scrubbed message, and 
         +reports only requests answered 500, never an error outside a re
         +quest (Q1 to Q3 of spec 007). A `SENTRY_DSN` must use `https://
         +`, except for a loopback host (`127.0.0.1`, `[::1]` or `localho
         +st`) such as the tests' fake endpoint.                         
      79 +- **Both off by default.** `docker compose up` stays exactly as
         + DEP-AC01 states, the demo and the tests need no key or network
         +, and no data leaves the machine unless the operator asks for i
         +t: the profile with `--profile observability` or `make observab
         +ility`, error reporting by setting `SENTRY_DSN`, which `compose
         +.yaml` never passes: the override `compose.error-reporting.yaml
         +` adds it to the replicas as `${SENTRY_DSN:?...}` and refuses t
         +o start without one. Prometheus publishes no port; Grafana's pa
         +nel "Replicas scraped" shows its targets.

         +beside the load test on CI's 2-CPU runner, and the suite checks
         + that port 3030 is free before it starts.                      
      93  - The error-reporting allowlist and scrub are code to maintain,
           and a scrubbed message can hide a value that would have helped
           a diagnosis.

          .

      177 +## Observability profile                                      
      178 +                                                              
      179 +An optional Compose profile adds Prometheus and Grafana to the
          + local stack; `docker compose up` never starts it ([ADR-0023](
          +docs/adr/0023-optional-observability-off-by-default.md), secti
          +on 1.9 of [`specs/008-deployment`](specs/008-deployment/spec.m

      180 +                                                              
      181 +```sh                                                         
      182 +make observability        # or: docker compose --profile obser
          +vability up --build --wait                                    
      183 +open http://localhost:3030 # Grafana, anonymous and read-only 
      184 +make down                 # stops the stack and the profile, k
          +eeping the database volume                                    
      185 +```                                                           
      186 +                                                              
      187 +Prometheus scrapes `/metrics` of `api-1` and `api-2` on `METRI
          +CS_PORT` inside the compose network every 5 seconds and publis
          +hes no port, so `METRICS_PORT` stays internal. Grafana, on `12
          +7.0.0.1:3030` only, lets anonymous visitors view and nobody si
          +gn in or change anything, and calls nothing outside the machin
          +e. Its dashboard shows, from the metrics above: the replicas s
          +craped, requests and errors by status class, p50, p95 and p99 
          +latency of the `/v1` routes, money movements by kind and outco
          +me, lock timeouts, idempotent replays, rate-limited requests a
          +nd pool usage. Run `npm run load` or `make load` against it to
          + watch the stack under load.                                  
      188 +                                                              
      189 +## Error reporting                                            
      190 +                                                              
      191 +Off by default: the demo and the tests need no key and send no
          +thing outside the machine. With `SENTRY_DSN` set, each replica
          + reports every request answered 500 (`/problems/internal-error
          +`) to that Sentry-compatible endpoint (Sentry, self-hosted Sen
          +try or GlitchTip), written by the service's own client with No
          +de's `fetch` (section 1.10 of [`specs/007-security-ops`](specs
          +/007-security-ops/spec.md), ADR-0023). Nothing else is reporte
          +d: no 4xx, no 503, no error outside a request.                
      192 +                                                              
      193 +A report holds only the correlation id (`requestId`, which fin
          +ds the request's log lines), the route template, the method, t
          +he replica, the SQLSTATE when there is one, the error's type, 
          +its stack frames and its message, scrubbed of secrets, the req
          +uest's token and key, JWT-shaped tokens, UUIDs and every run o
          +f digits. It never holds a header (so neither `Authorization` 
          +nor `Idempotency-Key`), a query string, a body, a client addre
          +ss, an amount, an account id, a token or a secret. Reporting n
          +ever changes or delays an answer: a send is abandoned after 2 
          +s, at most 20 reports wait per replica, and a failure is dropp
          +ed and logged once when sending starts failing and once when i
          +t works again.                                                
      194 +                                                              
      195 +`compose.yaml` never passes `SENTRY_DSN`, so a value left in t
          +he shell or a stray `.env` sends nothing. To turn it on for th
          +e local stack, add the override file, which refuses to start w
          +ithout one:                                                   
      196 +                                                              
      197 +```sh                                                         
      198 +SENTRY_DSN=https://<public key>@<host>/<project id> \         
      199 +  docker compose -f compose.yaml -f compose.error-reporting.ya
          +ml up --build --wait                                          
      200 +```                                                           
      201 +                                                              
      202 +The DSN must use `https://`, except for `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]` o
          +r `localhost`. In AWS error reporting stays off: the tasks hav
          +e no outbound internet path (`docs/deployment/aws.md`).       
      203 +

  ⎿  Updated docs/deployment/aws.md (+3 -1)
      126  
      127  Storage is watched through RDS events, because storage autosca
           ling grows the volume long before free space runs out, so a th
           reshold on `FreeStorageSpace` would page during normal growth.
            An EventBridge rule sends the four events that say space real
           ly is running out: RDS-EVENT-0225 (allocated storage at 80% of
            the 100 GB maximum), RDS-EVENT-0224 (an autoscaling step woul
           d reach the maximum), RDS-EVENT-0223 (autoscaling cannot scale
           ) and RDS-EVENT-0007 (storage exhausted). An RDS event subscri
           ption cannot be used: it filters by category only, and the "lo
           w storage" category also holds RDS-EVENT-0089, which fires bef
           ore every autoscaling step, while 0223, 0224 and 0225 sit in t
           he broad "failure" and "notification" categories.
      128

          - no security group admits. Scraping them is the next step, out
          - of scope here: an AWS Distro for OpenTelemetry collector as a
          - sidecar, writing to Amazon Managed Service for Prometheus.   
      129 +The service's Prometheus metrics stay on `METRICS_PORT`, which
          + no security group admits. Scraping them is the next step, out
          + of scope here: an AWS Distro for OpenTelemetry collector as a
          + sidecar, writing to Amazon Managed Service for Prometheus. Lo
          +cally, the Compose profile `observability` scrapes them with P
          +rometheus and shows them in Grafana (ADR-0023).               
      130 +                                                              
      131 +Error reporting (section 1.10 of spec 007) stays off in AWS: t
          +he task definitions set no `SENTRY_DSN`, and the tasks have no
          + outbound internet path to reach an endpoint. Turning it on ne
          +eds a NAT gateway with an egress allow-list for the endpoint, 
          +and the DSN as a Secrets Manager secret injected like the othe
          +rs.

⏺ Everything else passed (142 tests, the load test among them). DEP-AC33 timed
  out waiting for the movements panel to show a rate above 0.

  The likely cause is how Prometheus computes rates. rate() needs two samples of
  a series. A counter series that first appears with this AC's own deposit has
  no earlier sample, so its rate stays 0. The stack's replicas were also
  restarted by DEP-AC11, which resets their counters. Let me confirm with the
  run's numbers before changing anything.

⏺ The 12b-observability phase is implemented and green. Nothing is committed
  yet; your next /ship picks it up.

  What changed
  - Error reporting (spec 007): SENTRY_DSN must use https://; plain http:// is
    accepted only for 127.0.0.1, [::1] or localhost. Unset or empty turns
    reporting off. The reporter is the service's own small client on Node's
    fetch, with no new dependency. It reports each 500, once, from logFailure
    (every 500 response is logged there), and sends nothing for any other
    answer.
  - Observability profile (spec 008): Prometheus 3.15.0 and Grafana 13.2.3,
    pinned by version and the digests I looked up in the registry. Prometheus
    publishes no port. Grafana is on 127.0.0.1:3030, anonymous and read-only,
    with the dashboard. There's a make observability target.
  - Override file: compose.error-reporting.yaml passes SENTRY_DSN to api-1 and
    api-2 as ${SENTRY_DSN:?...}. Without a DSN, Compose refuses: "required
    variable SENTRY_DSN is missing a value". compose.yaml never mentions
    SENTRY_DSN. The README documents both.
  - Approval recorded: in plan 000 section 11, the open-question rows of both
    specs, ADR-0023 (now Accepted, with its follow-ups marked done) and
    docs/dependencies.md. Specs 007 and 008 stay Approved.

  Proof with a fake endpoint (SEC-AC44, SEC-AC41, SEC-AC42)
  - Without SENTRY_DSN: with it unset or empty, no reporter is created, the
    global fetch is never called, and the fake endpoint receives nothing.
  - With it set: a 500 arrives once, carrying its correlation id and route. A
    withdrawal of "4321" sent with a token, an Idempotency-Key and a cookie
    produces a report containing none of the token, key, cookie, account id,
    user id, amount, secrets or passwords. The DSN never appears in the logs.
  - The tests can fail: with the report call removed, all three fail.

  Manual check on your stack
  - Targets: after docker compose --profile observability up -d, Prometheus's
    API inside its container reports scf-api api-1 http://api-1:9464/metrics up
    and the same for api-2.
  - Dashboard: after 75 s of mixed traffic, all nine panels loaded with data in
    Grafana. The Lock timeouts panel shows "No data" because no lock timeout has
    happened since the replicas started. Screenshots:
    - /var/folders/_8/cz4jn0n97bvg0b8f91llngr00000gn/T/claude-chrome-screenshots
      -DmQKXu/screenshot-1791546073610-0.jpg
    - /var/folders/_8/cz4jn0n97bvg0b8f91llngr00000gn/T/claude-chrome-screenshots
      -DmQKXu/screenshot-1791546082302-1.jpg

  Results
  - npm run check: 378 unit tests passed.
  - npm run test:integration: 340 passed.
  - Full e2e suite: 143 passed. DEP-AC33 ran after the load test and removed
    Prometheus and Grafana when it ended.
  - npm run trace -- --require unit,integration,e2e: exits 0, with all 237 ACs
    covered.

  Changes since the approval you should check
  - DEP-AC33's Given: the first e2e run failed it. Prometheus needs two samples
    of a counter to show a rate, and earlier e2e tests restart the replicas,
    which resets their counters. I added a warm-up to the Given: other users
    first send a deposit, its replay and an over-balance withdrawal straight to
    each replica, and the test waits until Prometheus has scraped them. It then
    passes.
  - SEC-AC41's wording: C2's account creation counts against its rate limit, so
    its eighth read (its ninth request) is the one answered 429. The AC now says
    so.
  - "JWT-shaped" in section 1.10: now means the first segment starts with eyJ.
    Without that, any a.b.c text, such as a file name, would be scrubbed.
  - Grafana settings: besides the ones in the AC, I also turned off the news
    feed, Gravatar and plugin pre-install, so it calls nothing outside the
    machine.
  - Smaller changes:
    - make down now also stops the profile.
    - DEP-AC12's pin check accepts a v before the version, because Prometheus
      tags as v3.15.0.
    - The "error reporting is off" line is written once the app is ready, so a
      failed startup still writes exactly one line.
  - docs/performance.md: the e2e load test rewrote it with this run's slightly
    different latencies. I restored the committed version.

  Your dev stack is back up without the profile; make observability brings
  Grafana back.
